- **scrumia-compose**: Inspects, modifies, or diagnoses the module composition of a ScrumIA project. - **scrumia-design-audit**: Audits an existing interface against the design system — the drift (invented values, duplicated components) and, on equal footing, the mutedness (consistent but with no identity). Use it before a redesign, when plugging this module into an existing app, or to take stock after a phase of rapid growth. ([tibs245/scrumia](https://github.com/tibs245/scrumia/tree/HEAD/plugins/scrumia-design/skills/scrumia-design-audit))
- **scrumia-design-setup**: Installs the design system in a project — the design/ tree, its tokens and identity files, and the optional link to a Claude Design project. Invoked by scrumia-init when this module fills the design slot, or by hand to check the installation. ([tibs245/scrumia](https://github.com/tibs245/scrumia/tree/HEAD/plugins/scrumia-design/skills/scrumia-design-setup))
- **scrumia-design-sync**: Syncs the local design system with a claude.ai/design project through the DesignSync tool — component by component, never as a wholesale replace. Use it to publish a new or changed component for review, or to check what upstream has that the repo doesn't. ([tibs245/scrumia](https://github.com/tibs245/scrumia/tree/HEAD/plugins/scrumia-design/skills/scrumia-design-sync))
- **scrumia-design-system**: The ScrumIA design-system reference — where identity, tokens and components live, and how to consult them before writing an interface. Load it before creating or modifying a component, a style, or anything a user will look at. ([tibs245/scrumia](https://github.com/tibs245/scrumia/tree/HEAD/plugins/scrumia-design/skills/scrumia-design-system))
- **scrumia-effect**: The typed-effect discipline for an agent — describe before execute, failure as a value, the effect boundary, and which approach to reach for (Result, Either, IO/suspend, or effect.website). Library-agnostic on the pattern; cites effect.website as the reference implementation rather than wrapping it. Load before writing or reviewing code that produces, transforms, or consumes a side effect in an app where this module is plugged in. ([tibs245/scrumia](https://github.com/tibs245/scrumia/tree/HEAD/plugins/scrumia-effect/skills/scrumia-effect))
- **scrumia-extend**: The ScrumIA extension protocol — how a main skill opens a register, how any module contributes directives to it as pure data, and how scrumia-extends renders the table an agent picks from. Load it before writing or changing a module's extends.json, registers.json or dependencies.jsonl, or before adding an extension point to a skill. ([tibs245/scrumia](https://github.com/tibs245/scrumia/tree/HEAD/plugins/scrumia-core/skills/scrumia-extend))
- **scrumia-feature**: Creates, updates or audits a ScrumIA feature in features/. Applies the contextual angle catalog (index, business, qa, changelog, ux, tech, api-contract, archi, legal, security, devx) instead of a fixed template. Use it whenever a spec needs to be written, modified or checked. ([tibs245/scrumia](https://github.com/tibs245/scrumia/tree/HEAD/plugins/scrumia-specs/skills/scrumia-feature))
- **scrumia-functional-programming**: The ScrumIA Functional Programming paradigm — purity, total functions, referential transparency, immutability, composition over inheritance, and effect discipline, all in language-neutral terms so the same wording holds in Kotlin, JavaScript, Rust and any language that supports them. Load it before writing code in an app that extends this module. ([tibs245/scrumia](https://github.com/tibs245/scrumia/tree/HEAD/plugins/scrumia-functional-programming/skills/scrumia-functional-programming))
- **scrumia-gradle**: The ScrumIA Gradle reference — Kotlin DSL over Groovy, the version catalog as the one place versions live, the shape of a convention plugin (build-logic composite, precompiled script plugin), lazy task configuration, the build and configuration caches, composite builds for local siblings, pluginManagement, and documentation tasks wired into the lifecycle. Stated in terms of Gradle, not Kotlin Multiplatform. Load it before writing or reviewing a Gradle build script. ([tibs245/scrumia](https://github.com/tibs245/scrumia/tree/HEAD/plugins/scrumia-gradle/skills/scrumia-gradle))
- **scrumia-gradle-audit**: Audits an existing Gradle project against the eight rules this module ships — Kotlin DSL over Groovy, version catalog usage, convention plugin shape, lazy task configuration, the build and configuration caches, composite builds for local siblings, pluginManagement, and documentation tasks wired into the lifecycle. Use it before adopting the module on an existing codebase, when a build shape change ships, or to verify a project held to the rules really is. ([tibs245/scrumia](https://github.com/tibs245/scrumia/tree/HEAD/plugins/scrumia-gradle/skills/scrumia-gradle-audit))
- **scrumia-init**: Installs or verifies a project's ScrumIA composition — creates .scrumia/config.yaml, the specs tree, and writes into CLAUDE.md which modules are plugged in and where. Use it on first installation, or after adding or changing a module. ([tibs245/scrumia](https://github.com/tibs245/scrumia/tree/HEAD/plugins/scrumia-core/skills/scrumia-init))
- **scrumia-kotlin**: The ScrumIA Kotlin reference — naming and scope functions, null-safety and platform types, coroutines and Flow, data/sealed/value classes, object vs companion vs top-level, and visibility modifiers. Load it before writing Kotlin in an app whose language module is scrumia-kotlin, and read the refusal rules the same way on review. ([tibs245/scrumia](https://github.com/tibs245/scrumia/tree/HEAD/plugins/scrumia-kotlin))
- **scrumia-kotlin-audit**: Audit a Kotlin codebase against the six rule families this module ships — val/var and scope functions, null-safety and platform types, coroutines and Flow, data/sealed/value classes, object vs companion vs top-level, and visibility modifiers. Use it before adopting the module on an existing codebase, when a Kotlin bug ships, or to check a contract the language documents is actually being honoured. ([tibs245/scrumia](https://github.com/tibs245/scrumia/tree/HEAD/plugins/scrumia-kotlin/skills/scrumia-kotlin-audit))
- **scrumia-kotlin-multiplatform-mobile**: The Kotlin Multiplatform Mobile reference — six rule families that govern how KMP source sets, targets, Cocoapods integration and Gradle wiring compose into one multiplatform module. Use it before changing an expect/actual declaration, the source-set layout, the targets, a pod, or the KMP-shaped Gradle block. ([tibs245/scrumia](https://github.com/tibs245/scrumia/tree/HEAD/plugins/scrumia-kotlin-multiplatform-mobile/skills/scrumia-kotlin-multiplatform-mobile))
- **scrumia-material3**: The Material 3 entry point for an Android or Kotlin Multiplatform Mobile app — Compose is the default, Views is an explicit choice, and every token, component, theming and accessibility rule is stated as a falsifiable directive. Load before writing UI in an app that extends this module. ([tibs245/scrumia](https://github.com/tibs245/scrumia/tree/HEAD/plugins/scrumia-material3))
- **scrumia-module-audit**: Audits one ScrumIA module against the anatomy rules a program cannot decide from its tree alone — one concern per file, an index that routes rather than carries, and the README sections that depend on what a module actually does. Use it after `scrumia-module check`, or wherever a module's shape needs a verdict a reader would otherwise have to produce by hand. ([tibs245/scrumia](https://github.com/tibs245/scrumia/tree/HEAD/plugins/scrumia-core/skills/scrumia-module-audit))
- **scrumia-place**: Routes something learned during a run to exactly one destination — a module, this project, a feature, a ticket, the change itself, or agent memory. Use it when a run turns up something worth keeping, when an agent has just written to its own memory, or to re-place entries already sitting in a memory directory. ([tibs245/scrumia](https://github.com/tibs245/scrumia/tree/HEAD/plugins/scrumia-core/skills/scrumia-place))
- **scrumia-project-setup**: Sets up a project's GitHub tracking — board columns, labels, issue templates. Invoked by scrumia-init when this module fills the tracker slot, or by hand to check the installation. ([tibs245/scrumia](https://github.com/tibs245/scrumia/tree/HEAD/plugins/scrumia-github-project/skills/scrumia-project-setup))
- **scrumia-reactjs**: The ScrumIA React 19 reference — Server Components by default, Actions and useActionState for mutations and forms, derived state calculated during render, behaviour-first component tests, feature-based structure, and what we refuse. Load it before writing code in an app whose implementation module is scrumia-impl-reactjs. ([tibs245/scrumia](https://github.com/tibs245/scrumia/tree/HEAD/plugins/scrumia-impl-reactjs/skills/scrumia-reactjs))
- **scrumia-reactjs-audit**: Measures the gap between an existing React 19 app and scrumia-impl-reactjs rules — wrong client/server boundaries, useEffect for derived state, imperative DOM, components that fetch directly instead of going through the data boundary. Use it before plugging the module into an existing app, or to check that an app stays on the rails. ([tibs245/scrumia](https://github.com/tibs245/scrumia/tree/HEAD/plugins/scrumia-impl-reactjs/skills/scrumia-reactjs-audit))
- **scrumia-refine**: Refines a backlog ticket until it is ready for development — calls on the roles with the global view, splits into sub-issues when necessary, updates the specs. - **scrumia-review**: Reviews a ScrumIA PR by routing to the right reviewers according to what it touches. Use it to review an open PR, or before approving a merge. ([tibs245/scrumia](https://github.com/tibs245/scrumia/tree/HEAD/plugins/scrumia-github-project/skills/scrumia-review))
- **scrumia-rules**: The rules-hierarchy format reference — section anatomy (index, guides, decisions, section.json), how an agent navigates it without reading everything, and when a single file is enough instead. Load it before reading or writing any module-shipped or project-local rule section. ([tibs245/scrumia](https://github.com/tibs245/scrumia/tree/HEAD/plugins/scrumia-rules/skills/scrumia-rules))
- **scrumia-rules-setup**: Scaffolds a project-local rule section under .scrumia/rules/&lt;section&gt;/ — short interview, then harvests existing conventions from the codebase instead of inventing them, writes the index/guides/decisions, and registers the section in CLAUDE.md. Use it when a project's own conventions on a topic are worth writing down as rules. ([tibs245/scrumia](https://github.com/tibs245/scrumia/tree/HEAD/plugins/scrumia-rules/skills/scrumia-rules-setup))
- **scrumia-rules-update**: Evolves an existing rule — locates its governing decision, challenges it in writing against the new context, updates decision and guides together, and logs the change in the section's CHANGELOG. Use whenever a rule needs to change, not just its guide. ([tibs245/scrumia](https://github.com/tibs245/scrumia/tree/HEAD/plugins/scrumia-rules/skills/scrumia-rules-update))
- **scrumia-rust**: The ScrumIA Rust reference — tests per invariant, unrepresentable invalid states, typed errors per layer, structure by domain, and what we refuse. Load it before writing code in an app whose implementation module is scrumia-impl-rust. ([tibs245/scrumia](https://github.com/tibs245/scrumia/tree/HEAD/plugins/scrumia-impl-rust/skills/scrumia-rust))
- **scrumia-rust-audit**: Measures the gap between an existing Rust app and the scrumia-impl-rust rules — unwrap in production, untyped errors, traits without polymorphism, unjustified unsafe. Use it before plugging the module into an existing app, or to check that a well-kept app stays that way. ([tibs245/scrumia](https://github.com/tibs245/scrumia/tree/HEAD/plugins/scrumia-impl-rust/skills/scrumia-rust-audit))
- **scrumia-solid-audit**: Audits an app's design against SOLID — the violations and, on equal footing, the over-applications (abstractions without variation, dead indirections). ([tibs245/scrumia](https://github.com/tibs245/scrumia/tree/HEAD/plugins/scrumia-solid-principles/skills/scrumia-solid-audit))
- **scrumia-solid-principles**: The ScrumIA SOLID reference — the five principles, each with its limit of application, in OO and functional alike. Load it before writing or designing code in an app where this module is plugged in. ([tibs245/scrumia](https://github.com/tibs245/scrumia/tree/HEAD/plugins/scrumia-solid-principles/skills/scrumia-solid-principles))
- **scrumia-solid-refactor**: Resolves a design finding — SOLID violation or over-application — in safe, verifiable steps. ([tibs245/scrumia](https://github.com/tibs245/scrumia/tree/HEAD/plugins/scrumia-solid-principles/skills/scrumia-solid-refactor))
- **scrumia-solidjs**: The ScrumIA SolidJS reference — behaviour-first component tests, fine-grained reactivity without React reflexes, feature-based structure, and what we refuse. Load it before writing code in an app whose implementation module is scrumia-impl-solidjs. ([tibs245/scrumia](https://github.com/tibs245/scrumia/tree/HEAD/plugins/scrumia-impl-solidjs/skills/scrumia-solidjs))
- **scrumia-solidjs-audit**: Measures the gap between an existing SolidJS app and scrumia-impl-solidjs rules — destructured props, effects used as derivations, components that fetch, structure by kind. Use it before plugging the module into an existing app, or to check that an app stays on the rails. ([tibs245/scrumia](https://github.com/tibs245/scrumia/tree/HEAD/plugins/scrumia-impl-solidjs/skills/scrumia-solidjs-audit))
- **scrumia-specs-find**: Navigates a project's specs — finds the feature that owns a rule, traces dependencies between features, or loads the minimal context useful to a ticket. - **scrumia-specs-setup**: Installs the specs tree in a project — features/business and features/app per application.
